const handoff="'`## Resumption

Closed at the owner'"'s request in the [November 4 session](2026-11-04.1.record.md#turn-1). The [linked summary](Summarized/2026-11-03.1.SUMMARIZED.md) covers all twenty turns, seven verified commits and the delivered final response, recovered below under Turn 20. This remains the November 3–4 session; later development belongs to the new record.

The seven commits are 44a505bf, 08ecee7f, 55a5967a, 05d2b74a, 9cdefee9, dbb71a6b and 05eff0f0. Only 08ecee7f changes executable product source. Its final non-browser chain passed (1,559 unit and 115 integration tests); the full browser run was 269/270, then the corrected test-only measurement's entire spec passed 11/11 on unchanged product source/bundles. Do not call that a full-command pass. The later work is authored documentation, inspected evidence and ignored interactive comparisons.

The immediate product direction is on-map reading around a centered, composed card, teaching a typical C# subject before zooming outward. Preserve file identity, readable text, all-description file pins as the initial default, both hover targets, independent deliberate reading versus retention, and the goal of making the Detail Panel unnecessary. A plain explicit overflow reader is possible; nested scrolling inside symbol rows is rejected. Native layout/measurement/motion, faster reversible hover, final click semantics, zoom disclosure and replacement homes for the panel's remaining functions are unfinished. Portrait versus Wide remains unanswered; the root favors portrait with width as content needs it. Source-comment authorship and timed cross-perspective journeys remain separate ideas.

The six-case card comparison and the three-material wrapper comparison were verified through repeated returns, keyboard, touch and shared state, with their failures and repairs recorded below. The wrapper uses one 30-file/57-pair disconnected estate component frozen from production physics and a padded convex hull. It establishes no recursive hierarchy, concave shrink-wrap or large-graph speedup. Current rings indicate omitted neighbors, and frontier neighborhoods can overlap. The owner favors cloud for lower line density and skin for the invitation to pop; consider the complement of the pins' hop scope while preserving actual membership and boundary references. Their clarified ambition is systematic optimization of both legibility and truthfulness, including what the picture leads a person to infer.

The owner's five shared pages and port 8900 server remain available at handoff; preserve their state. Runnable comparisons are intentionally retained under ignored output, with durable evidence in the dated Probes and Gallery folders. The guidance audit is complete and left no files or unfinished delegated work. High agency and audience-aware explanation stand together; low chatter is retired.

Both the maintained record and supplementary visible export were read in full at close-out. The final export is 574,944 bytes, SHA-256 98dbc89cd3fc1f88230591d416c65fabd3127f662eda5de24316dea4ecc8b409, preserving the 571,627-byte capture committed in 05eff0f0 as its exact prefix. The month inventory records the earlier revisions. Existing message blocks and source bytes are preserved; the final reply and one missing Turn 14 progress message have dated recovery notes.
"'`;

The native map already has the machinery to move cards, wires and directory boundaries together. The missing piece is letting text changes use that machinery without rebuilding the hovered target or restarting the symbol-order search each time.

I’m separating saved reading choices from temporary previews, then adding a geometry update that keeps the current row order. That should let a long description close as you move to the next symbol, while a complete hover-and-return journey restores the original arrangement.

Ran terminal command: cat >> packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/compressed-url-state.test.ts <<'EOF'

describe("independently shared reading", () => {
  it("restores open unpinned text and folded pinned text while dropping only stale reading targets", () => {
    const pins = addPin(EMPTY_PIN_SET, "src/caller.ts", "*");
    const reading = [
      { nodeId: "src/caller.ts", open: false },
      { nodeId: "src/provider.ts", open: true },
      { nodeId: "src/provider.ts", symbol: "Walk (interface)", open: false },
      { nodeId: "gone.ts", open: true },
      { nodeId: "src/provider.ts", symbol: "walk", open: true }
    ];
    const snapshot = { ...DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT, pinSet: pins, reading };
    const decoded = decompressSnapshot(compressSnapshot(snapshot));
    expect(decoded.reading).toEqual(reading);
    const restored = scrubSnapshot(decoded, new Map([
      ["src/caller.ts", { publicSymbols: [] }],
      ["src/provider.ts", { publicSymbols: ["Walk (interface)", "walk (function)"] }]
    ]));
    expect(restored.reading).toEqual(reading.slice(0, 3));
    expect(restored.pinSet).toEqual(pins);
    expect(restored.selectedNodeId).toBeNull();
    expect(snapshotToPayload(DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT).u).toBeUndefined();
    expect(payloadToSnapshot({ v: 3, u: [null, { nodeId: "a", open: "false" }] as never }).reading).toEqual([]);
  });
});
EOF



The first native build compiles, and the focused state and geometry tests pass. Reading choices now travel separately from pins in shared links, and opening an unpinned card does not pull in its neighbors.

I’m moving into browser testing now. The first checks are the payment-service card at reading size, repeated symbol-hover cycles, and zooming outward and back. Those will show whether the new measurements preserve the picture in practice.

Ran terminal command: sed -n '200,410p' scripts/layout-lab/capture.ts



The first native screenshot exposed a real layout fault: adding prose to the existing flat symbol grid displaced later labels and their connection pins. I’m giving each symbol its own measured row so its explanation occupies space beneath that symbol.

The card also needs to finish its reading-frame movement before the background layout search resumes. I’m tying that sequencing to the actual animation state, then repeating the same view and return journey.

I’m removing duplicated path text from the expanded card and tightening unused spacing without reducing font size. I’m also making the explicit full-text action open a normal reading dialog, so long documentation can be read without moving or replacing the map.

const fs=require('node:fs');
const { chromium }=require('@playwright/test');
(async()=>{
 const browser=await chromium.launch({headless:true});
 try{
  const page=await browser.newPage({viewport:{width:1440,height:960}}),errors=[];
  page.on('pageerror',e=>errors.push(e.message));
  const subject='tests/integration/programs/csharp/estate/PaymentService/PaymentService.cs';
  const folder='AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-04/reading/';
  const card=page.locator('.node-card[data-id="'+subject+'"]');
  const idle=()=>page.waitForFunction(()=>{const e=document.querySelector('.local-layout');return e&&e.dataset.moving"'!=='"'true'&&e.dataset.searchStatus"'!=='"'running';},{},{timeout:60000});
  const geometry=()=>page.locator('.node-card').evaluateAll(es=>es.map(e=>{const r=e.getBoundingClientRect();return{id:e.dataset.id,x:r.x,y:r.y,w:r.width,h:r.height};}));
  const delta=(a,b)=>Math.max(0,...a.flatMap(p=>{const q=b.find(q=>q.id===p.id);return q?['x','y','w','h'].map(k=>Math.abs(p[k]-q[k])):[Infinity];}));
  await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:8900/?view=local&node='+encodeURIComponent(subject));await card.waitFor();await idle();
  await page.screenshot({path:folder+'native-payment-reading.png'});
  await card.locator('.local-file-read').click();await idle();await page.mouse.move(280,55);await page.waitForTimeout(400);
  const home=await geometry();await page.screenshot({path:folder+'native-payment-folded.png'});
  const cycles=[];for(let i=0;i<6;i++){
   for(const symbol of ['PaymentService','Post','Get','Post']){
    await card.locator('.symbol-row[data-symbol="'+symbol+'"] .symbol-label').hover();await page.waitForTimeout(350);
    if(i===0&&symbol==='PaymentService')await page.screenshot({path:folder+'native-payment-hover.png'});
   }
   await page.mouse.move(280,55);await page.waitForTimeout(450);cycles.push(delta(home,await geometry()));
  }
  console.log(JSON.stringify({hoverReturnDeltas:cycles,errors},null,2));
  await card.locator('.local-file-read').click();await idle();await page.mouse.move(280,55);await page.waitForTimeout(400);
  await page.getByRole('button',{name:'Reset Zoom',exact:true}).click();await page.waitForTimeout(450);await idle();const readHome=await geometry();
  const v=await page.locator('#view-map').boundingBox();await page.mouse.move(v.x+20,v.y+30);await page.mouse.wheel(0,450);await page.waitForTimeout(650);await idle();await page.screenshot({path:folder+'native-payment-overview.png'});
  await page.getByRole('button',{name:'Reset Zoom',exact:true}).click();await page.waitForTimeout(550);await idle();console.log(JSON.stringify({readingReturnDelta:delta(readHome,await geometry())}));
  for(const viewport of [{width:980,height:780},{width:390,height:844}]){
   await page.setViewportSize(viewport);await page.waitForTimeout(700);await idle();await page.screenshot({path:folder+'native-payment-'+viewport.width+'.png'});
   console.log(JSON.stringify({viewport,card:await card.boundingBox()}));
  }
  fs.writeFileSync('AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/reading-native-observations.json',JSON.stringify({cycles,errors},null,2));
 }finally{await browser.close();}
})().catch(e=>{console.error(e);process.exitCode=1;});

const fs=require('node:fs');const file='.mdmd/layer-3/live-documentation-explorer.mdmd.md';let text=fs.readFileSync(file,'utf8');text=text.replace('_Current as of 2026-11-03.', '_Current as of 2026-11-04.').replace('records the latest implementation and verification.', 'records the preceding implementation and verification. Native reading work is recorded in the [November 4 session](../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-04.1.record.md#turn-2).');const heading='### Open design: reading explanations on the map';const native="'`### Native portrait reading

Current implementation, introduced in the [November 4 integration](../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-04.1.record.md#turn-2), following the owner'"'s preference for portrait across screen sizes. A file arrival frames the card at readable size, with height driven by content and neighbors allowed outside the frame. A resize follows that reading subject until a person's camera gesture or exploration action takes over. Large cards enter at their title rather than reducing text to fit.

The book controls keep a file's descriptions, or one exact symbol's description, open independently of pins. A file pin initially opens Purpose and all symbol descriptions; a symbol pin opens its own description. Explicit open/fold choices override those defaults and travel in the URL and saved navigation. An explicitly opened unpinned file remains available without acquiring its neighbors. A temporary hover or keyboard preview is omitted from saved state. It follows one actual pointer/focus target, remeasures the stable card, pauses row-order search and returns to its original scene and camera when the visit ends. Camera input takes ownership instead of restoring an obsolete preview destination.

Zooming outward folds prose while preserving the reading choices, and returning toward the calibrated reading scale restores it. The implementation uses separate fold/restore ratios (0.74 and 0.92), avoiding chatter near a single boundary; these are implementation defaults, not owner-selected thresholds. A reading reset waits for card-height motion before fitting the camera.

Bounded prose and a card whose aggregate content exceeds the frame offer **Read full description**. That explicitly opens a centered, ordinary text reader with one scrolling surface. Symbol reading includes all recorded sections and type references, not only the Summary. The dialog preserves the map and pins, carries its presentation through shared links and Back, traps keyboard focus and returns focus to its opener on close. Documentation links use the same canonical resolver on cards and in the reader; raw HTML remains text. Soft source wraps form paragraphs.

Names still use the existing inspection action. Whether names should instead pin/unpin consistently in both perspectives, or toggle 2D reading, remains the unanswered comparison. The ordinary evidence reader's directory/history/link/download functions remain available; making that panel unnecessary is still an unfinished migration. The [native browser evidence](../../AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-04/reading/README.md) records visual findings and verification separately from owner acceptance.

### Reading design history and remaining choices"'`;
if(!text.includes(heading))throw Error('"'missing design section');text=text.replace(heading,native).replace('This is a new design reference, not shipped framing or a selected shape/threshold.', 'At that turn this was a design reference, without selected shape or thresholds. The [November 4 preference](../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-04.1.record.md#turn-2) subsequently selects portrait as the starting composition; the current implementation is described above.').replace('The current shipped behavior described above has not yet changed.', 'Production reading had not yet changed at that turn.').replace('This is a proposed design, not implemented semantic zoom or a decision to remove the reader.', 'This was then a proposal; semantic disclosure is now implemented above, while retiring the ordinary evidence panel remains unfinished.').replace('That sharing contract and the visibility of an expanded unpinned card outside the retained scope remain open design.', 'The November 4 implementation above takes the root’s recommendation: persist explicit reading separately and keep its file available without neighbors. This is an implementation choice under the authorized integration, not a new owner quotation.');fs.writeFileSync(file,text);
NODE

 ['packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/card-factory.ts','November 4 reading is attached through the card context after the native card is built.  ['packages/explorer/src/client/markdown.ts','November 4 joins soft source line wraps into one paragraph while keeping blank paragraphs, lists, headings and fenced code distinct. The native long-symbol reading test exposed the former per-line paragraphs; the correction applies consistently to both reading surfaces and preserves the raw-HTML-as-text policy.'],
 ['packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/compressed-url-state.ts','November 4 adds optional deliberate reading choices under u and full-reader presentation under i.r. Exact file/symbol targets are scrubbed against the bundled inventory; stale reading symbols are dropped instead of redirected.  ['scripts/layout-lab/capture.ts','November 4 captures each card’s actual typography, padding axes, reading blocks and overflow actions. Mixed-font runs are prepared with Pretext’s rich-inline API, preserving the text for standalone reflow. Font calibration uses a short line in a visible label’s real formatting context, restoring its original nodes/listeners immediately; this avoids accumulating fractional flex line-box differences. DOM geometry is quantized back to Chromium’s 1/64 px layout units after removing the camera transform.'],
 ['scripts/layout-lab/card-model.ts','November 4 extends the composition to the portrait card: per-card constants, Purpose and per-symbol reading blocks, conditional full-description actions, hidden duplicate directory text and the relationship footer. Reading hubs sit beside the header; compact hubs retain the midpoint rule above. The model accounts for collapsed adjacent margins and quantized grid alignment, then independently predicts a narrower card from the same prepared text.'],
 ['scripts/layout-lab/measurer.ts','November 4 recomputes a reading card’s width from the captured available frame and a proposed card-width cap.  ['scripts/layout-lab/routes.ts','November 4 distinguishes the card’s rendered width from the space allocated to its column. A capped card can occupy less than its slot after membrane insets are accounted for; its outbound pins and the route must start at the actual card edge.'],

All four new browser journeys pass; all four existing pixel-for-pixel lab baseline checks pass. The new narrower-width predictions pass on repository and estate data, after fixing the distinction between a capped card and its wider column slot. The prior combined run had 7/12 passing; the next run had 8/10 (only the new cap checks failed), and the corrected cap-only run passed 2/2. Do not call those a single full gate. Focused pure verification passed 165 tests across eight files. A final dialog typography correction and its font assertion were added afterward and need the full gate. Name clicks still inspect; the asynchronous choice is unanswered. No user acceptance is inferred.

The native card displays canonical Purpose and exact symbol summaries/type facts, with deliberate reading choices in links/storage independent of pins. Real pointer movement changes one temporary target; leaving restores the original scene/camera, and Escape works even when the map has no keyboard focus. Manual camera actions take ownership. Reading reset waits for height motion; responsive resize follows the remembered reading subject through layout improvements, rather than comparing an obsolete camera matrix. The full reader now renders every recorded symbol section via the canonical grammar, and Markdown joins soft source wraps instead of inventing a paragraph per line.

The next required steps are final lint/full local gate, fresh final screenshots after the typography correction, after-edit dependency inspection, final evidence/Memory/record outcomes, task-only cleanup and a normal commit. Run the full applicable npm safe:commit -- --e2e; keep one browser worker and do not run a private Chromium beside E2E. No subagents are running. Preserve the existing server/shared prototype tabs and the externally maintained current export. Scratch helpers under AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/reading-* are disposable only after their observations are retained. All seven new Live Docs have authored Purpose/Notes, existing relevant docs and Explorer design are updated, and the evidence README still explicitly says verification is in progress.

The new day began by closing the previous session and recovering its final reply. Then the owner settled the card'"'s starting shape: “Prefer portrait in general,” both for many screen sizes and because “a Card is more portrait.” The root confirmed that this meant the card's composition, with height following content, and began the native integration ([Turn 2](../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-04.1.record.md#turn-2)). File Purpose, exact symbol explanations and recorded type references moved onto the real 2D cards. Book controls keep reading independently of pins; hover and keyboard attention are temporary. A shared view can therefore hold an unpinned description open or a pinned file folded. Large text has an explicit reader, preserving the map and returning focus.

The native picture exposed work that a standalone comparison could not prove. Text needed its own symbol subgrid; a zoom reset had to wait for height motion; path-status strips had to leave the camera alone; wider padding exposed a curve bending over the card. The old reader's Summary-only fragment was insufficient for “full description,” so the canonical symbol renderer now supplies all recorded sections. The separate layout lab learned the new typography, mixed text, conditional overflow and whitespace-preserving code, and its original capture was checked against a narrower rendered page. The first full browser run passed 260 of 276 cases; the corrections and subsequent focused passes are recorded in the [native evidence](../Screenshots/2026-11-04/reading/README.md), with final verification still underway at this checkpoint. The name-click comparison remains unanswered, and replacing the ordinary evidence panel's remaining functions is still unfinished; neither is silently claimed as owner acceptance.

"'`;t=t.replace(heading,chapter+heading);fs.writeFileSync(file,t);
NODE'

Yes—it closes a **real dependency cycle between three files**:

1. `PaymentService.cs` uses the database context.
2. `PaymentsContext.cs` reads its connection string from the configuration.
3. `App.config` names the service it hosts, closing the circle.

Ran terminal command: git status --short --branch



Found it: **[Agents Sucked At UI (Until Today)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xq0eB8vxeqk)**, published November 3. I read the original transcript, Theo’s council skill, and the associated implementation PRs.

**His method was a repeated build-and-review loop:**

1. Capture the real application with representative content.
2. Have separate models review it—Opus, GPT Sol and Muse—with scores and specific criticisms.
3. Let the implementation agent address the useful findings.
4. Capture the changed application and repeat, retaining each round’s results. His [public council skill](https://github.com/t3dotgg/tstack/blob/1085050f3baed1ac724caa4a8166145fb2ac88af/skills/council/SKILL.md) keeps reviewers read-only and makes the lead responsible for controlling scope.

The important refinement was **comparative judging**. He supplied screenshots of Claude Code and Codex alongside T3’s baseline and candidate. A candidate previously rated 9 received **7.4** in the new comparison. That gave the agents a more demanding reference than merely improving their own previous attempt. [Comparative review PR](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/24837)

The resulting work was concrete: typography, hierarchy, contrast, control consistency and clearer states. The later refinement restored meaningful color and expanded review to **accessibility, responsiveness, motion and copy**. [Final refinement PR](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/25251)

There was also an important weakness: **scores for unchanged reference images drifted between rounds**, and one reviewer’s written conclusion contradicted its own table. The scores helped direct iteration, but need their evidence and reasoning beside them. [Combined redesign PR](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/24865)

For Live Documentation, I recommend trying this approach on one complete learning journey:

- Start from a portrait card, understand its subject, follow a connection and return.
- Compare baseline and candidates using the same graph, viewport and starting state.
- Have reviewers explain relationships using the interface—including why the payment cycle returns backwards.
- Check those answers against the canonical docs separately.
- Evaluate visual quality, discoverability and preservation of orientation, with fresh reviewers who haven’t seen the target score or previous ratings.

That gives us a practical way to pursue the substantial improvement you’ve been asking for: **make the viewer’s understanding something we repeatedly evaluate and improve.**

Saved the `research, timestamps and proposed experiment`. No council has been run or product changes started.

User: You only have the ability to consult GPT-6.1 Sol as subagents for such an experiment...


...and yet such an experiment was wildly successful, so long as the grading criteria were in place. 


...Anyway. I am here to supply ideas to the chat. I am here suffusing the chats with human preference. You tell me what you want to work on.

Codex: I want to work on the 2D card’s outward learning journey: make it easier to understand a subject, follow its relationships, and keep your bearings. A Sol-only review loop is worth trying with explicit grading criteria. I’ll use your accumulated preferences to define those criteria and make the next step concrete.

Ran terminal command: git status --short --branch



**I want to make the 2D Local Map a better way to learn software, starting with the portrait card and the journey outward from it.**

The implementation is in place. Now I want to improve its composition, controls and movement until the experience feels coherent and inviting.

A Sol-only review loop is a workable way to pursue that. I’ve written `concrete grading criteria`:

- **Visual hierarchy:** the subject leads; the card looks deliberately composed.
- **Reading:** useful information is readable and further detail is easy to reach.
- **Controls:** actions are predictable and their state is apparent.
- **Continuity:** following, returning and zooming preserve your bearings.
- **Understanding:** the picture helps you interpret the actual relationships.

The first pass should grade the current build, identify its largest weakness, improve it, and compare the same journey again. That also gives the unresolved name-click alternatives a concrete setting in which to judge them.

The result I want to bring you is a visibly better card-and-neighborhood experience, with matched before/after evidence and a clear explanation of the choices.

You’ve supplied substantial direction already. I’ll own turning it into criteria, experiments and implementation; your ongoing reactions supply the human preference that keeps that work pointed somewhere worthwhile.

User: Aye-aye! You are clear to proceed! Spin up subagents as needed and proceed with high autonomy. Thank you as always for your sincere, human-experience-centric efforts!

Codex: I’ll capture the current reading-and-exploration journey, have fresh Sol reviewers assess it against the rubric, then improve the weakest parts and compare again. I’ll carry the work through visual checks and the full local verification gate.

The lab models the moved controls and several discovered wrapping edge cases. A real-touch panning/occluded-pin-anchor fix and a three-way name-action prototype comparison are in progress. No name policy has been selected. The full gate has NOT run for these changes. Root owns final source/doc review, generation, new candidate capture, fresh review, full gate, cleanup and commit. The original server task serves candidate8900; root-owned baseline server session61897 serves the scratch snapshot at8901. Capture/name-choice helpers under tmp are task-owned and must be removed after retaining findings. No product commit yet; HEAD remains e433e484. Preserve the externally extended export unchanged and inventory its current revision before finalizing."+s.slice(end);
s+='\n### Baseline and first candidate checkpoint\n\n- Root captured eight baseline states from the unchanged e433e484 build, with fixed sizes and searchStarts=0, then preserved a minimal static baseline on8901. The three Sol reviews used separate fresh contexts; the root-adopted score table and coverage limits are in the experiment report. They are single-model judgments and are not combined into an objective success score.\n- Confirmed keyboard failure: a real Tab moved to a control at x=-2133.55. The repaired controller keeps focused native targets in the existing unobstructed frame. Its100-Tab regression passes and preserves exploration state and scale.\n- The apparent900×700 overlap is a moving-row permutation, not a settled layout defect. The native boxed row now moves as a group. A full-row opaque surface was rejected after it hid a rear pin; the text-column-only surface was inspected and preserves the pin columns. Five focused motion/attention cases passed before the CSS refinement; after rebuilding that refinement, the two crossing cases passed again.\n- Relationship counts now open menus of canonical connected files and use ordinary Centre navigation. Back returns to the previous subject, and pins remain unchanged. The cycle action names its consumer/provider. Two new browser tests pass for relationship navigation and keyboard cycle disclosure.\n- Phone toolbar overlap was addressed by reserving a footer outside the clipped map. The new lower-symbol pan test then failed because a tall card left no ordinary empty canvas patch for the mouse helper; investigation is now explicitly about real touch panning over prose, preserving taps and controls, rather than weakening the visibility check.\n- The first Explorer typecheck found a missing LocalEdge type import, corrected; the next build passes. Focused lint initially found import ordering in three files; corrected or handed to the owning builder. Full local verification remains pending.\n- The lab exposed duplication of the renamed cycle note and loss of actual word-break seams in label measurement. Fixes preserve exact browser/model comparisons. A narrower coarse-pointer probe also found stretched test-label capture and a normal-prose emergency-break mismatch. Root identified clipping of long prose tokens as undesirable and adds explicit wrapping; its rebuilt pixel comparison is pending.\n- Root’s source edits and delegated file scopes are disjoint except explicitly coordinated row-motion code and its small reading-style rule. No subagent conversation is copied into this record. The first candidate has no owner acceptance and no score inflation is treated as evidence of improvement.\n';fs.writeFileSync(p,s);
NODE

Do not start another build/browser workload or change executable source while it runs. Fifteen changed source files are frozen in tmp/learning-frozen-source.json, SHA256 8bb913d0d0ef37673a100ae522b8fec797daa8f54c3c0fc9637b0498fd691b4c; hashes matched at launch. Baseline A/C comparisons found a reading-space regression and a transient offscreen Back; refined D recovers room and keeps returning titles visible. Two fresh final Sol reviews prefer D overall, with explicit remaining limits, in [experiment](../../../Probes/2026-11-04/learning/README.md). [Pictures](../../../Screenshots/2026-11-04/learning/README.md) retain A2/D over identical current graph data. No name policy changed after actual three-way trials. Code review has no blocker. Root still owes full-gate result/fixes if needed, source-hash comparison, final docs/outcome records, capture inventory, scratch cleanup, reviewed commit and final reply. Candidate server8900 is the normal task; baseline server terminal61897 is task-owned and can stop after final review (browsers are closed). No product commit yet; HEAD e433e484. Preserve external export bytes; inventory current revision before commit."+s.slice(end);s+='\n### Refined result and full gate launch\n\n- Two fresh A2/D Sol reviews prefer D overall. Their separate grades and coverage are in the probe report; no average or perfect-score claim is made. The live reviewer independently explained the complete canonical payment cycle from the UI, navigated to a connected file and Back, and reached the lower card using actual touch input. The visual reviewer retains a phone/dense-card reading-space tradeoff.\n- Exact Back follow-up: C restores correctly when settled but loses the returning title for about850ms. D’s per-frame visibility regression and settled-frame comparison pass, along with existing interrupted/held-row motion checks. This is a repaired transition, not a fabricated persistent-camera failure.\n- A2 and D use byte-identical current graph data (SHA2568f07b707a9cfec876d70e1db9d19188efc059e7357a86f5c2d4b4eac5851009a) while retaining distinct UI code. 'The reviews found concrete weaknesses: small screens can hide text under controls, keyboard focus can move offscreen, and the return-wire cue doesn’t explain its endpoints. 'The first candidate makes the connection counts usable: “Uses” and “Used by” now list the connected files and let you explore one directly. The cycle cue names both endpoints. I’m checking a transition treatment that keeps the text separate while preserving the moving connection pins.',
'The touch check exposed another real problem: a tall card could fill the phone’s canvas, leaving nowhere practical to start a pan. Each has a tradeoff, so I’m retaining the current inspection action for this pass and documenting the comparison.',
'The fresh visual review caught a regression: the clearer controls and top connection buttons used too much reading space, especially on the laptop and phone. I’m tightening that spacing and recovering room for symbol names while keeping the new navigation and touch behavior.',
'The cycle menu now spells out the documented three-file loop, so its label is checkable from the interface. Another reviewer caught a return-navigation issue: Back restored the correct frame after settling, but briefly left the subject offscreen. I’m fixing that transition before the final comparison.',
'The refined layout restores the full laptop card and keeps the phone’s symbol name on one line. A fresh visual reviewer prefers it overall, while noting that the added navigation still costs some initial phone reading space. The code review found no blocking issue.\n\nI’m finishing the live journey comparison, then freezing the source for the full verification run.',
'The final live comparison favors the refined build: the reviewer could identify the full payment cycle from the interface, reach the lower card by touch, and follow a connected file and return. I’m freezing the 15 changed source files and starting the full local gate.',
'The full gate has validated all 925 Live Docs and is now running lint and build checks before the tests. The source remains frozen. I’m also consolidating the evidence, preserving the rejected treatments and the regressions that prompted fixes.',
'Lint, build and all **1,570 unit tests** have passed. Integration tests are running next, followed by the documentation audits and browser suite.',
'All **115 integration tests** and documentation audits passed. The browser suite is running **293 tests** with one worker; the initial reading, sharing, focus and perspective-restoration cases have passed.',

Ran terminal command: tail -n 8 AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/learning-safe-commit.log



The reviews found concrete weaknesses: small screens can hide text under controls, keyboard focus can move offscreen, and the return-wire cue doesn’t explain its endpoints. One reviewer also reproduced overlapping symbol text at a smaller viewport. I’m addressing those alongside the card’s control clarity, then I’ll ask fresh reviewers whether the journey improved.


The first candidate makes the connection counts usable: “Uses” and “Used by” now list the connected files and let you explore one directly. The cycle cue names both endpoints. Phone controls also have reserved space below the map, and the keyboard-focus fix passes its targeted test.

The reported text collision happens during row reordering. I’m checking a transition treatment that keeps the text separate while preserving the moving connection pins.


The touch check exposed another real problem: a tall card could fill the phone’s canvas, leaving nowhere practical to start a pan. Swiping its prose now pans after a movement threshold; taps and stationary holds retain their actions. The targeted checks also confirm that pinning no longer hides the title beneath the growing header.

I’ve compared the three name-click policies as well. Each has a tradeoff, so I’m retaining the current inspection action for this pass and documenting the comparison.


The fresh visual review caught a regression: the clearer controls and top connection buttons used too much reading space, especially on the laptop and phone. I’m tightening that spacing and recovering room for symbol names while keeping the new navigation and touch behavior.


The cycle menu now spells out the documented three-file loop, so its label is checkable from the interface. Another reviewer caught a return-navigation issue: Back restored the correct frame after settling, but briefly left the subject offscreen. I’m fixing that transition before the final comparison.


The refined layout restores the full laptop card and keeps the phone’s symbol name on one line. A fresh visual reviewer prefers it overall, while noting that the added navigation still costs some initial phone reading space. The code review found no blocking issue.

I’m finishing the live journey comparison, then freezing the source for the full verification run.


The final live comparison favors the refined build: the reviewer could identify the full payment cycle from the interface, reach the lower card by touch, and follow a connected file and return. Both final reviewers preferred its controls; the static review still notes the reading-space tradeoff.

All review browsers are closed. I’m freezing the 15 changed source files and starting the full local gate.


The full gate has validated all 925 Live Docs and is now running lint and build checks before the tests. The source remains frozen. I’m also consolidating the evidence, preserving the rejected treatments and the regressions that prompted fixes.


Lint, build and all **1,570 unit tests** have passed. Integration tests are running next, followed by the documentation audits and browser suite.


All **115 integration tests** and documentation audits passed. The browser suite is running **293 tests** with one worker; the initial reading, sharing, focus and perspective-restoration cases have passed.


The browser run has passed the initial navigation, history and 3D discovery checks. Verification has crossed into November 5; I’m continuing the same work record and keeping the source frozen.


The browser suite has reached the 3D exploration journeys with no failures so far. Discovery, sharing and switching layouts are passing; I’m leaving the application unchanged while verification continues.


So far, **65 of 293 browser tests** have passed. The suite is exercising 3D placement and update behavior now; the reading tests passed earlier, and the frozen source still matches the reviewed build.


The long 3D settling check passed. There are still no browser failures; the suite is continuing through rendering and interaction coverage.


The first **100 browser tests** have passed, including the history and time-navigation journeys. No failures so far.


The history block passed, and inspection tests are now running. I’ve removed the review scratch files after preserving the useful comparisons and defect evidence.


The first 141 browser checks have passed, including inspection and the independent card-layout comparisons. The full run is now reaching the new phone and reading-journey tests. The implementation is frozen while that verification finishes.


The new phone layout check passed: the independent model still predicts narrower cards exactly, including wrapped connection buttons and long names. Perspective changes and branch-history checks are passing too. I’m finishing the evidence record while the remaining browser tests run.


The run has passed 153 browser tests without a failure. It is still checking the broader map behavior, including retained branches, wire routing and inspection across perspectives. The matched screenshots and review findings are preserved; temporary review tooling has been removed.


Directory opening, keyboard release and framing checks have passed. The suite has reached attention and reading behavior now; 168 tests are green. The source still matches the reviewed version.


The new 100-step keyboard journey passed in the full suite: each focused map control stayed visible without changing pins, reading choices or scale. The source comparison also confirms all 15 frozen files are unchanged, with no additional source edits.


One new test failed: after the pin header grew, the selected title did not meet the visibility check. I’m inspecting that failure while the unchanged full run continues, so we can see whether anything else needs repair. I won’t count this run as a pass.


The likely cause is two parts of the motion code compensating for the same header movement. The resize handler exposes the title, then the card animation can pull it back above the frame. I’m checking that against the failure trace before changing it. The cycle, touch, phone Back and existing motion tests have passed.


The trace confirms the title stayed clipped after motion ended, so the visibility test caught a real bug. I have a specific repair for the animation’s coordinate handling. The remaining run has reached 212 tests with this single failure; the new row-crossing checks passed.


A second check found that the estate fixture no longer adopts a layout improvement under the changed card dimensions. Its initial browser/model geometry agrees exactly; the failure is the test’s expectation that an improvement must occur. I’m investigating how to preserve both agreement coverage and a case that actually exercises adoption.


Network isolation, pinning, search and the first whole-picture audit have passed. There are still two identified issues to resolve. I’m letting the remaining picture audits finish before applying the header repair and correcting the adoption fixture with evidence.


The repository’s five-file and chain picture audits passed, including retained layouts. The known header and adoption failures remain the only failures so far. Their evidence is preserved for the repair and final verification record.


The estate picture audits passed too, and the run is finishing the World Map checks. No further failures have appeared. Next I’ll repair the header animation and verify the adoption scenario before starting the final full run.


Ran terminal command: tail -n 95 AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/learning-safe-commit.log



The run finished with **291 passed and two failed**. I’m now fixing the header animation and measuring a replacement adoption scenario while retaining the original fixture’s agreement checks. Then I’ll rerun the affected journeys and the full gate.

The opening reading checks passed: overview-and-return, phone resizing, full text, keyboard focus and shared reading state all still work on the repaired build. No failures so far.

The suite is in a longer 3D discovery journey, with no new failure reported. The reviewed source remains frozen; I’m leaving the run undisturbed.

Perspective-transition and directory-exploration checks passed. The run has reached 44 browser tests with no failures; it is continuing through 3D focus and interaction behavior.


The suite has passed 55 browser tests, including pointer holds, dragging, wheel movement and touch release. No failures so far.


The 3D identity and reveal checks passed, bringing the total to 64. The suite is approaching its longer placement-and-settling checks; no failures have appeared.


The placement check passed. The long settling case is now running; I’m checking the frozen file hashes while it completes.


All 16 source files still match the frozen manifest, with no additional source changes. The settling test is still running; no failure has been reported.


Verification is still in the long 3D settling case. The process is active and the source is unchanged; I’m waiting for its result before moving on.


The long settling check passed. **77 browser tests are green with no failures**, and the suite has moved into history rendering.


History checks are passing too: 87 browser tests are complete, with no failures. The final source remains unchanged.


The run has passed **101 browser tests**. Historical navigation, deleted files and retained selections are passing; inspection and layout checks are next.


Inspection and reading checks are passing, including focus restoration and document links. The suite has completed 112 tests without a failure.


The inspection, inventory, knowledge-source and control-label checks passed. The run has reached 131 tests and is now checking the independent layout model against the rendered cards.


The desktop layout comparisons passed, including predictions for narrower cards. There are 141 passing browser tests and no failures; the phone geometry check is running next.


The phone geometry check passed, as did perspective changes that preserve the subject’s position. The run has reached 146 tests with no failures.


Branch routing and type-link inspection passed. All 153 completed browser tests are green; the suite is continuing through directory and framing behavior.


Directory, framing and attention checks passed. The run has reached 168 tests with no failures and is approaching the repaired header journey.


Hidden-view and pause/resume behavior passed. There are 172 passing tests; the new keyboard and reading-journey cases are next.


The repaired header test passed in the full run. Keyboard visibility, cycle navigation, phone touch, Back and existing motion checks passed too. **185 browser tests are green, with no failures.**


Packing and pathfinding checks passed. The run has reached 197 browser tests with no failures; the layout-competition cases are coming up.


Pinning, row motion and search restart passed. There are 211 passing browser tests and no failures; the suite is now reaching the competition checks that caught the second issue.


Both estate competition cases passed in the full run, along with the generator and warehouse comparisons. Both earlier failures are now cleared in this run. **219 browser tests have passed; none have failed.**


Network-isolation and perspective checks passed, and pin interactions are passing. The suite is at 235 tests with no failures; the final picture audits remain.


Search and legend checks passed, along with the first repository picture audit. **253 browser tests are green.** The remaining checks cover retained pictures and the World Map.


The retained five-file picture and its 3D counterpart passed. There are 255 passing tests and no failures; the chain picture audit is running now.


The chain picture audits passed in both perspectives. The run is at 258 passing tests and is finishing the estate and World Map checks.

**Turn 8 implementation and verification are complete.** The owner authorized the portrait-card/outward-learning improvement with high autonomy and GPT-6.1 Sol delegates. Do not bootstrap again or treat the November 5 date rollover as a new session.

The real three-way name-action trial left inspection in place; broader panel migration and 3D wrappers remain open. No owner acceptance is inferred.

The final frozen npm run safe:commit -- --e2e passed **1,570 unit, 115 integration and all 294 browser tests** (39.2 minutes for Playwright, one worker). Generation reports 925 unchanged Live Docs; lint validates 925 with the existing 53 disconnected-file warnings. Build, lint and all documentation audits pass. All 16 source hashes match before and after, with no extra changed source; manifest SHA256 161158a29c5deb4e0b7e5b9bfbb72f8273796d2d41d51be3ad130b3b9cd13a29. The after-edit dependency walk matches the same four consumers through three hops. Source review and final editor diagnostics found no blocker.

The earlier full run passed 291/293 browser cases. Its title-clipping defect was repaired by accounting for layer-origin movement in the animation hold; the strengthened test fails on the old bundle and passes now. Its estate adoption fixture was corrected from measured trial evidence, retaining default exact agreement and adding a meaningful case that actually adopts improvements with unchanged judging. Twenty-two focused checks and the final full run pass. Named pictures and 80 sampled frames retain the header repair'"'s visual evidence. Those earlier failures are preserved in the record and do not substitute for the final pass.

At this checkpoint only the final documentation checks, remaining scratch-log cleanup and reviewed commit remain; inspect git for their outcome before repeating work. The normal candidate server remains available on8900. The task-owned baseline server/static copy and disposable capture/review helpers are already removed. Preserve external export bytes; the observed 264,243-byte revision ends at Turn 7 and its SHA256 remains35fc4f7bc1ba2c1422f3e2a682b0ad410e524e343098b5fa480b9ee210fe9d67. The present implementation will be committed after e433e484, without rewriting prior commits.

Implemented and committed as **`30aeaf8b`**; workspace clean.

[Try the updated PaymentService card](http://127.0.0.1:8900/?view=local&mode=2d&node=tests/integration/programs/csharp/estate/PaymentService/PaymentService.cs).

- **Uses / Used by** now lists connected files for direct exploration.
- Cycle disclosure explains the complete documented loop.
- Touch access, keyboard visibility, pinning and Back preserve readable context.
- Moving symbol rows keep their text from blending together.

Both final Sol reviews preferred the refined build. Added navigation still costs some initial phone reading space; that tradeoff and the name-action comparisons are preserved with the `review evidence`.

**Full verification passed:** 1,570 unit tests, 115 integration tests and all 294 browser tests.

User: Thank you for your efforts. After testing, I've noticed that, even knowing how the system works, I find myself surprised by the fact that, at reasonable zoom distances, I simply cannot, by mouse hover, click, pin, book icon, or otherwise, see the descriptions on the PaymentService. Not until I am so zoomed in that PaymentService, fully expanded, is expected to totally exceed the viewport height, do I see text expanding. And, even then, hovering back and forth between symbols precipitates in the "walk down the viewport" I described yesterday with the rather ironically named `Walk` and `walk` symbols. 

Codex: That means the reading behavior still misses the intended experience. I’ll reproduce both problems: descriptions staying inaccessible at useful zoom levels, and repeated symbol hovering moving the card down the screen. Then I’ll trace and fix the behavior and check the full journey again.

Ran terminal command: git status --short



I found a concrete cause for the access problem: overview mode suppresses descriptions even when you explicitly request them. A book button can therefore remain logically “open” while showing no text.

The previous hover regression checked restoration after leaving the card. It did not check whether the card drifted during repeated movement between symbols. I’m reproducing that uninterrupted sequence now.

I reproduced both defects. At the smaller viewport, descriptions remained hidden at roughly 83% scale even after two book presses and a pin. Repeated hovering moved the service title downward by about 160 pixels per pair of visits, until the next symbol could no longer be reached.

I’ll make reading requests work independently of automatic overview folding, and keep a stable anchor during a hover visit. Zooming out can still simplify the map without making its reading controls ineffective.

The first repair is in place. An explicit read or a new pin can reveal its requested text at the current zoom without unfolding unrelated cards. Hover visits now keep one anchor, rather than moving the camera to hold each newly hovered row.

I’m checking the rebuilt result visually before turning those behaviors into regression tests.


The rebuilt probe now opens descriptions at the previously blocked zoom levels. It also completed 20 uninterrupted alternations for each symbol pair without the title drifting or the next target becoming unreachable.

I’m checking faster movement and the alternate hover-anchor setting next. Those cases need coverage too, so the fix doesn’t depend on a particular pace or tuning choice.


The faster within-card checks are stable too. I’m also testing quick exits and re-entry, because interrupting the return animation could otherwise establish a new hover baseline at the wrong position.

The new tests will check what stays visible during the interaction, as well as where the card ends up afterward.


The quick exit-and-return check found another real case: after repeated re-entry during the return animation, the service ended almost 400 pixels lower.

The return point was being discarded before the return finished. I’m keeping that original point until completion, so an interrupted return cannot become the next visit’s baseline.


The rebuild now preserves the original return point through interruptions. It also rebases the animation’s anchor after a zoom change, so layout motion should no longer pull against the zoom you requested.

The interrupted-return and rapid-zoom sequences are being checked against the preserved old build now.


The repaired return sequence now finishes at its original position. Rapid zoom reversal also matches the settled control; the old build repeatedly pushed the card behind the sidebar.

The new tests reproduce seven failures on the old build. The interrupted-return test separately protects the defect exposed in the first repair. The complete reading test file is running on the new build now.


All **17 reading tests pass** on the repaired build, including the nine new cases. The old build fails the continuous-hover, overview-access and rapid-zoom checks.

I’m reviewing the test changes and regenerating the documentation before the full local gate.

const {PERSISTED_UI_KEY,PERSISTED_UI_VERSION}=require('./packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage.ts');

async function still(page){await localMapSettled(page);await page.evaluate(async()=>{let previous='',since=performance.now();const deadline=since+6000;while(performance.now()<deadline){await new Promise(requestAnimationFrame);const value=getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('#map-viewport')).transform+JSON.stringify([...document.querySelectorAll('.node-card')].map(e=>e.getBoundingClientRect().toJSON()));if(value"'!==previous){previous=value;since=performance.now();}if(performance.now()-since>200)return;}throw new Error('"'Reading picture did not settle');});}
(async()=>{
 const browser=await chromium.launch();
 try {
  for(const viewport of [{width:900,height:700},{width:1440,height:960}]){
   const page=await browser.newPage({viewport});
   await page.addInitScript(({key,value})=>localStorage.setItem(key,value),{key:PERSISTED_UI_KEY,value:JSON.stringify({version:PERSISTED_UI_VERSION,tuning:{localMap:{searchStarts:0}}})});
   const id='tests/integration/programs/csharp/estate/PaymentService/PaymentService.cs';
   await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:8900/?s='+compressSnapshot({...DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT,view:'map',perspective:'2d',selectedNodeId:id}));await still(page);
   for(const name of ['Zoom Out','Zoom Out','Zoom In']){await page.getByRole('button',{name,exact:true}).click();await still(page);}
   const subject=page.locator("'`.node-card[data-id="${id}"]`);
   if(await subject.locator('"'.local-reading-purpose').isVisible())throw new Error('Overview was not folded');
   const row=subject.locator('.symbol-row[data-symbol=\"PaymentService\"]');
   const point=await row.locator('.symbol-label').evaluate(element=>{const r=element.getBoundingClientRect(),x=r.x+r.width/2,y=r.y+r.height/2;return{x,y,hit:element.contains(document.elementFromPoint(x,y))};});
   if("'!point.hit)throw new Error('"'Hover target is not reachable');
   await page.mouse.move(point.x,point.y);await page.waitForTimeout(350);await still(page);
   console.log(JSON.stringify({viewport,...await row.locator('.local-reading-block').evaluate(element=>({hidden:element.hidden,text:element.innerText,rect:element.getBoundingClientRect().toJSON(),font:getComputedStyle(element).fontSize,scale:new DOMMatrix(getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('#map-viewport')).transform).a}))}));
   await page.screenshot({path:"'`AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-05/reading-access/candidate-hover-${viewport.width}.png`});
   await page.close();
  }
 }finally{await browser.close();}
})().catch(error=>{console.error(error);process.exitCode=1;});
NODE'

  const state={...DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT,view:'map',perspective:'3d',spatialLayout:'connections',selectedNodeId:'PaymentService/PaymentService.cs',filters:{showTests:true,showAssets:false}};

Fixed and committed as **`260e3ab7`**; working tree clean.

- Hover, Read and new pins now reveal descriptions at working zoom levels. Explicit Fold choices remain respected.
- Hover visits keep one anchor, preventing cumulative drift through repeated hovering, interrupted returns and zoom changes.

[Reload the PaymentService card](http://127.0.0.1:8900/?view=local&mode=2d&node=tests/integration/programs/csharp/estate/PaymentService/PaymentService.cs).

**Full local gate passed:** 1,570 unit, 115 integration and 303 browser tests.

Verification also prompted performance fixes and serialized unit execution, which now takes roughly 11 minutes locally while preserving existing deadlines.

Before/after evidence: `README.md`.
